A numeral annotated\n/// `(n : Nat)` is its constructor term.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  /// Zero.\n  | Zero : Nat\n  /// A positive number.\n  | Positive : (p : Pos) --> Nat\n\n/// A carrier a natural literal reads into: a literal checked where `Carrier`\n/// is expected is `fromNat` of its primitive integer.\npub theory FromNat where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromNat : (n : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n/// A carrier a negative literal reads into: `-n` checked where `Carrier` is\n/// expected is `fromNeg` of the magnitude `n`, at the operand type `fromNat`\n/// takes.\npub theory FromNeg where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromNeg : (n : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n/// A carrier a decimal literal reads into: a decimal checked where `Carrier`\n/// is expected is `fromDecimal` of its exact digits `mantissa` and its\n/// `exponent`, the value `mantissa × 10^exponent`, as Lean's `OfScientific`.\n/// A decimal at `f64` reads as the float it rounds to, through no model.\npub theory FromDecimal where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromDecimal : (mantissa : Integer, exponent : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n//! The data subject's shared declarations, written once and imported by every\n//! source that reads them (`import \"./data-base.gandr\";`): the free naturals,\n//! their addition and the numerals two and three, the integers presented by two\n//! cancelling constructors, a three-colour enumeration, the two branches of a\n//! binary node and the polymorphic lists. A data block is an inductive type\n//! given by its constructors [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory], and a\n//! rule between constructor terms quotients it\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]. The universe subject\n//! reads the naturals and the branches from here too.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]\n//! - cites: [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]\n//!\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-89366-2_16 \"Altenkirch et al., Quotient inductive-inductive types\"\n//! [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]: https://archive-pml.github.io/martin-lof/pdfs/Bibliopolis-Book-retypeset-1984.pdf \"Martin-Löf, Intuitionistic Type Theory\"\n\n/// Natural numbers, free: every constructor term is its own value.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  | Zero : Nat\n  | Succ : (n : Nat) --> Nat\n\n/// Addition by recursion on the second argument, the recursive call nested\n/// in the successor it returns.\npub def rec plus(n : Nat, m : Nat) -> Nat =\n  match m\n    | Zero => n\n    | Succ(p) => Succ (plus[<] n p)\n\n/// Two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ Zero)\") ]\npub def two : Nat = Succ (Succ Zero);\n\n/// Three.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\npub def three : Nat = Succ two;\n\n/// The integers as a presented type: a successor and a predecessor that\n/// cancel, each rule invertible.\n@[ normalizes(\"S(P(S(Z))) ~> S(Z)\") ]\npub data Int : Type where {\n  | Z : Int\n  | S : (x : Int) --> Int\n  | P : (x : Int) --> Int\n  rule : S (P x) <=> x;\n  rule : P (S x) <=> x;\n}\n\n/// Three colours: an enumeration, a data type whose constructors take no\n/// field.\npub data Color : Type where\n  | Red : Color\n  | Green : Color\n  | Blue : Color\n\n/// The two branches of a binary node.\npub data Two : Type where\n  | L : Two\n  | R : Two\n\n/// Lists of elements of a type `a`. The element stands only where a list\n/// holds it, strictly positive, so a family nests through it.\npub data List(a : Type) : Type where\n  | Nil : List(a)\n  | Cons : (head : a, tail : List(a)) --> List(a)\n\n//! The folds over a polymorphic list — its length, appending and mapping — in\n//! the spellings an arm admits, each as the sugared form writes it: a `run`\n//! that only sequences a call, its result returned or the `return` elided, is\n//! written with the call nested in the constructor it returns. A structural\n//! recursive call stands one stage below the list it was read from\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]; the list is the polymorphic one\n//! of Martin-Löf's type theory. A fold may take the element type implicitly,\n//! `[a : Type]`: its recursive call omits `a` and passes the function's own.\n//! They run at the naturals in `recursive-nat-lists.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals and the lists.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n/// The length of a list, returned, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length_elided(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_elided[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the recursive call nested in the successor.\ndef rec length_nested(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_nested[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, its element type implicit: the recursive call omits\n/// `a`, so it passes the function's own, and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec length_implicit[a : Type](xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_implicit[<] rest)\n\n/// Appending two lists, returned, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append_elided(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_elided[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the recursive call nested in the constructor.\ndef rec append_nested(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_nested[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, their element type implicit: the recursive call\n/// passes `a` and `ys` on and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec append_implicit[a : Type](ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_implicit[<] ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, returned, the runs written at their reads.\npub def rec map(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, the `return`s elided, the runs written at\n/// their reads.\ndef rec map_elided(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> List(b))\n\n//! Rose trees: a node holds its children, in two forms. Written directly, a\n//! node holds a `List` of nodes: the list's parameter is strictly positive, so\n//! each child stands below the node and the family declares; a measure recurses\n//! through the list's `map`, which a call instantiates at the children's stage,\n//! and a run passes `map` the children's type as a code. Written as a block, a\n//! node holds a forest and a forest is empty or a tree before a forest: the two\n//! families name each other, so they are one block, admitted as one and sharing\n//! one stage; and the two measures name each other, so they are one fixpoint\n//! over the sum of their domains, each measure that fixpoint at its own\n//! summand. A measure may take a parameter after the tree, and the fixpoint\n//! passes it on. A call through either stands one stage below the node or\n//! forest it was read from [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]. [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]. The universe subject\n//! reads the naturals and the branches from here too.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]\n//! - cites: [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]\n//!\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-89366-2_16 \"Altenkirch et al., Quotient inductive-inductive types\"\n//! [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]: https://archive-pml.github.io/martin-lof/pdfs/Bibliopolis-Book-retypeset-1984.pdf \"Martin-Löf, Intuitionistic Type Theory\"\n\n/// Natural numbers, free: every constructor term is its own value.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  | Zero : Nat\n  | Succ : (n : Nat) --> Nat\n\n/// Addition by recursion on the second argument, the recursive call nested\n/// in the successor it returns.\npub def rec plus(n : Nat, m : Nat) -> Nat =\n  match m\n    | Zero => n\n    | Succ(p) => Succ (plus[<] n p)\n\n/// Two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ Zero)\") ]\npub def two : Nat = Succ (Succ Zero);\n\n/// Three.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\npub def three : Nat = Succ two;\n\n/// The integers as a presented type: a successor and a predecessor that\n/// cancel, each rule invertible.\n@[ normalizes(\"S(P(S(Z))) ~> S(Z)\") ]\npub data Int : Type where {\n  | Z : Int\n  | S : (x : Int) --> Int\n  | P : (x : Int) --> Int\n  rule : S (P x) <=> x;\n  rule : P (S x) <=> x;\n}\n\n/// Three colours: an enumeration, a data type whose constructors take no\n/// field.\npub data Color : Type where\n  | Red : Color\n  | Green : Color\n  | Blue : Color\n\n/// The two branches of a binary node.\npub data Two : Type where\n  | L : Two\n  | R : Two\n\n/// Lists of elements of a type `a`. The element stands only where a list\n/// holds it, strictly positive, so a family nests through it.\npub data List(a : Type) : Type where\n  | Nil : List(a)\n  | Cons : (head : a, tail : List(a)) --> List(a)\n\n//! The folds over a polymorphic list — its length, appending and mapping — in\n//! the spellings an arm admits, each as the sugared form writes it: a `run`\n//! that only sequences a call, its result returned or the `return` elided, is\n//! written with the call nested in the constructor it returns. A structural\n//! recursive call stands one stage below the list it was read from\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]; the list is the polymorphic one\n//! of Martin-Löf's type theory. A fold may take the element type implicitly,\n//! `[a : Type]`: its recursive call omits `a` and passes the function's own.\n//! They run at the naturals in `recursive-nat-lists.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals and the lists.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n/// The length of a list, returned, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length_elided(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_elided[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the recursive call nested in the successor.\ndef rec length_nested(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_nested[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, its element type implicit: the recursive call omits\n/// `a`, so it passes the function's own, and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec length_implicit[a : Type](xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_implicit[<] rest)\n\n/// Appending two lists, returned, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append_elided(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_elided[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the recursive call nested in the constructor.\ndef rec append_nested(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_nested[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, their element type implicit: the recursive call\n/// passes `a` and `ys` on and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec append_implicit[a : Type](ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_implicit[<] ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, returned, the runs written at their reads.\npub def rec map(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, the `return`s elided, the runs written at\n/// their reads.\ndef rec map_elided(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_elided[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping over a list, both calls nested in the constructor.\ndef rec map_nested(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_nested[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n//! Rose trees: a node holds its children, in two forms. Written directly, a\n//! node holds a `List` of nodes: the list's parameter is strictly positive, so\n//! each child stands below the node and the family declares; a measure recurses\n//! through the list's `map`, which a call instantiates at the children's stage,\n//! and a run passes `map` the children's type as a code. Written as a block, a\n//! node holds a forest and a forest is empty or a tree before a forest: the two\n//! families name each other, so they are one block, admitted as one and sharing\n//! one stage; and the two measures name each other, so they are one fixpoint\n//! over the sum of their domains, each measure that fixpoint at its own\n//! summand. A measure may take a parameter after the tree, and the fixpoint\n//! passes it on. A call through either stands one stage below the node or\n//! forest it was read from [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]. What\n//! the direct form refuses is in `../../pathological/data/rose.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - see: #3722 — a measure passes a parameter after the tree to its sibling,\n//!   by which `treeWeight` and `forestWeight` check\n//! - see: #517 — `Bush`\n//! - see: #393 — `nodes`\n//! - see: #452 — `shrub_nodes`, `grown_nodes`\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals the measures count in, their addition, and the lists the\n// direct form nests through.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n// The lists' `map`, which the direct form's measures recurse through.\nimport \"./recursive-lists.gandr\";\n\n/// The direct form: a node over a list of nodes, nested through `List`'s\n/// strictly positive parameter.\ndata Bush : Type where\n  | Sprout : (kids : List(Bush)) --> Bush\n\n/// A node without children, written directly.\ndef sprig : Bush = Sprout (Nil : List(Bush));\n\n/// A node over two childless nodes, written directly.\ndef shrub : Bush = Sprout (Cons sprig (Cons sprig (Nil : List(Bush)) : List(Bush)) : List(Bush));\n\n/// A list of numbers, summed.\ndef rec sum(xs : List(Nat)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => plus x (sum[<] rest)\n\n/// The nodes of a bush, its children's measured through `map`: the call\n/// instantiates `map`'s element at the children's stage, below the node's,\n/// so `nodes[<]` takes each child.\ndef rec nodes(t : Bush) -> Nat =\n  match t\n    | Sprout(kids) => Succ (sum (map Bush Nat nodes[<] kids))\n\n/// A map over the tree: every node gains a childless node beside its\n/// children, each child grown through `map` at its stage.\ndef rec grow(t : Bush) -> Bush =\n  match t\n    | Sprout(kids) => Sprout (Cons sprig (map Bush Bush grow[<] kids) : List(Bush))\n\n/// The shrub's three nodes, its children counted through `map` at the\n/// children's type.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\ndef shrub_nodes : () -> Nat = nodes shrub;\n\n/// The shrub grown, then counted: the root gains a childless node and each\n/// of its two children gains one, six nodes in all.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))))\") ]\ndef grown_nodes : () -> Nat = nodes (grow shrub);\n\n/// The tree and its forest, one block: each family names the other, and\n/// the two measures recurse through each other.\nrec {\n  /// A node over its forest of children.\n  @[ checks ]\n  data Rose : Type where {\n    | Node : (kids : Forest) --> Rose\n  }\n\n  /// The children of a node, in order.\n  @[ checks ]\n  data Forest : Type where {\n    | Empty : Forest\n    | More : (head : Rose, tail : Forest) --> Forest\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a tree: its own and its children's.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec treeSize(t : Rose) -> Nat {\n    match t\n      | Node(kids) => { let s = forestSize[<] kids; Succ s }\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a forest: each tree's, summed.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec forestSize(ts : Forest) -> Nat =\n    match ts\n      | Empty => Zero\n      | More(t, rest) => { let a = treeSize[<] t; let b = forestSize[<] rest; plus a b }\n}\n\n/// A node without children.\ndef leaf : Rose = Node Empty;\n\n/// A node over two leaves.\ndef sapling : Rose = Node (More leaf (More leaf Empty));\n\n/// The sapling's three nodes.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\ndef sapling_nodes : () -> Nat = treeSize sapling;\n\n/// A forest of a sapling and a leaf: four nodes.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))\") ]\ndef forested : () -> Nat = forestSize (More sapling (More leaf Empty));\n\n/// Two measures that pass a weight to each other unchanged, one block. Each\n/// descends on its first parameter, and the joint passes `k` on.\nrec {\n  /// The nodes of a tree, each weighing `k`.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec treeWeight(t : Rose, k : Nat) -> Nat {\n    match t\n      | Node(kids) => plus k (forestWeight[<] kids k)\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a forest, each weighing `k`.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec forestWeight(ts : Forest, k : Nat) -> Nat =\n    match ts\n      | Empty => Zero\n      | More(t, rest) => plus (treeWeight[<] t k) (forestWeight[<] rest k)\n}\n\n/// The sapling's three nodes, each weighing two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))))\") ]\ndef sapling_weight : () -> Nat = treeWeight sapling (Succ (Succ Zero));\n\n/// Two members that rebuild a tree and its forest, one block. Each member\n/// returns its own family, so the joint's goal differs from member to\n/// member, and a case over the sum computes it.\n///\n/// - see: #3880\nrec {\n  /// A copy of the tree, one node at a time.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec bumpTree(t : Rose) -> Rose {\n    match t\n      | Node(kids) => Node (bumpForest[<] kids)\n  }\n\n  /// A copy of the forest, one tree at a time.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec bumpForest(ts : Forest) -> Forest =\n    match ts\n      | Empty => Empty\n      | More(t, rest) => More (bumpTree[<] t) (bumpForest[<] rest)\n}\n\n/// The sapling's copy keeps its three nodes.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\ndef rebuilt_nodes : () -> Nat = treeSize (bumpTree sapling);\n\n/// The copy of a forest of a sapling and a leaf keeps its four nodes.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))\") ]\ndef rebuilt_forest : () -> Nat = forestSize (bumpForest (More sapling (More leaf Empty)));\n"}, grammar:{0:6328610752581770380}, nodes:size=5247, starts:size=102, roots:size=1, index:{forms:size=101, digests:size=101, ranges:size=101, ranks:size=5247, digest:Some({0:"\U00000011\xd1\"d\x9eZ\xcd\xfd\xf9،\U00000014\xc1\x81\U0000001b\U0000001b\U0000001eTR\xad\U00000004\x9c\x82\xf3\xba\U0000000f\xd3\xc3\xdf\r\xb0\x87"})}}, unit={sources:size=4, prelude:Some({0:0})}, arena={values:{frozen:None, own:size=0}, computations:{frozen:None, own:size=0}, value_types:{frozen:None, own:size=0}, comp_types:{frozen:None, own:size=0}, words:{words:{}}, region_binders_from:None}, lowerings={__0:3}, decisions={held:0, kept:size=0}) at compose.rs:1308:5

Rose trees: a node holds its children, in two forms. Written directly, a\n//! node holds a `List` of nodes: the list's parameter is strictly positive, so\n//! each child stands below the node and the family declares; a measure recurses\n//! through the list's `map`, which a call instantiates at the children's stage,\n//! and a run passes `map` the children's type as a code. Written as a block, a\n//! node holds a forest and a forest is empty or a tree before a forest: the two\n//! families name each other, so they are one block, admitted as one and sharing\n//! one stage; and the two measures name each other, so they are one fixpoint\n//! over the sum of their domains, each measure that fixpoint at its own\n//! summand. The folds over a polymorphic list — its length, appending and mapping — in\n//! the spellings an arm admits, each as the sugared form writes it: a `run`\n//! that only sequences a call, its result returned or the `return` elided, is\n//! written with the call nested in the constructor it returns. A structural\n//! recursive call stands one stage below the list it was read from\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]; the list is the polymorphic one\n//! of Martin-Löf's type theory. A fold may take the element type implicitly,\n//! `[a : Type]`: its recursive call omits `a` and passes the function's own.\n//! They run at the naturals in `recursive-nat-lists.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! length of a list, returned, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length_elided(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ length_implicit[a : Type](xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_implicit[<] rest)\n\n/// Appending two lists, returned, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the def rec append_implicit[a : Type](ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_implicit[<] ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, returned, the runs written at their reads.\npub def rec map(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, the `return`s elided, the runs written at\n/// their reads.\ndef rec map_elided(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> List(b))\n\n//! Rose trees: a node holds its children, in two forms. Written directly, a\n//! node holds a `List` of nodes: the list's parameter is strictly positive, so\n//! each child stands below the node and the family declares; a measure recurses\n//! through the list's `map`, which a call instantiates at the children's stage,\n//! and a run passes `map` the children's type as a code. Written as a block, a\n//! node holds a forest and a forest is empty or a tree before a forest: the two\n//! families name each other, so they are one block, admitted as one and sharing\n//! one stage; and the two measures name each other, so they are one fixpoint\n//! over the sum of their domains, each measure that fixpoint at its own\n//! summand. A measure may take a parameter after the tree, and the fixpoint\n//! passes it on. A call through either stands one stage below the node or\n//! forest it was read from [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]. A structural\n//! recursive call stands one stage below the list it was read from\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]; the list is the polymorphic one\n//! of Martin-Löf's type theory. A fold may take the element type implicitly,\n//! `[a : Type]`: its recursive call omits `a` and passes the function's own.\n//! They run at the naturals in `recursive-nat-lists.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals and the lists.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n/// The read.\ndef rec append(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the List(b))\n\n//! Rose trees: a node holds its children, in two forms. Written directly, a\n//! node holds a `List` of nodes: the list's parameter is strictly positive, so\n//! each child stands below the node and the family declares; a measure recurses\n//! through the list's `map`, which a call instantiates at the children's stage,\n//! and a run passes `map` the children's type as a code. Written as a block, a\n//! node holds a forest and a forest is empty or a tree before a forest: the two\n//! families name each other, so they are one block, admitted as one and sharing\n//! one stage; and the two measures name each other, so they are one fixpoint\n//! over the sum of their domains, each measure that fixpoint at its own\n//! summand. A measure may take a parameter after the tree, and the fixpoint\n//! passes it on. A call through either stands one stage below the node or\n//! forest it was read from [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]. What\n//! the direct form refuses is in `../../pathological/data/rose.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - see: #3722 — a measure passes a parameter after the tree to its sibling,\n//!   by which `treeWeight` and `forestWeight` check\n//! - see: #517 — `Bush`\n//! - see: #393 — `nodes`\n//! - see: #452 — `shrub_nodes`, `grown_nodes`\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! | Node : (kids : Forest) --> Rose\n  }\n\n  /// The children of a node, in order.\n  @[ checks ]\n  data Forest : Type where {\n    | Empty : Forest\n    | More : (head : Rose, tail : Forest) --> Forest\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a tree: its own and its children's.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec treeSize(t : Rose) -> Nat {\n    match t\n      | Node(kids) => { let s = forestSize[<] kids; Succ s }\n The data subject's shared declarations, written once and imported by every\n//! source that reads them (`import \"./data-base.gandr\";`): the free naturals,\n//! their addition and the numerals two and three, the integers presented by two\n//! cancelling constructors, a three-colour enumeration, the two branches of a\n//! binary node and the polymorphic lists. A data block is an inductive type\n//! given by its constructors [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory], and a\n//! rule between constructor terms quotients it\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]. The universe subject\n//! reads the naturals and the branches from here too.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]\n//! - cites: [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]\n//!\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-89366-2_16 \"Altenkirch et al., Quotient inductive-inductive types\"\n//! [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]: https://archive-pml.github.io/martin-lof/pdfs/Bibliopolis-Book-retypeset-1984.pdf \"Martin-Löf, Intuitionistic Type Theory\"\n\n/// Natural numbers, free: every constructor term is its own value.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  | Zero : Nat\n  | Succ : (n : Nat) --> Nat\n\n/// Addition by recursion on the second argument, the recursive call nested\n/// in the successor it returns.\npub def rec plus(n : Nat, m : Nat) -> Nat =\n  match m\n    | Zero => n\n    | Succ(p) => Succ (plus[<] n p)\n\n/// Two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ Zero)\") ]\npub def two : Nat = Succ (Succ Zero);\n\n/// Three.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\npub def three : Nat = Succ two;\n\n/// The integers as a presented type: a successor and a predecessor that\n/// cancel, each rule invertible.\n@[ normalizes(\"S(P(S(Z))) ~> S(Z)\") ]\npub data Int : Type where {\n  | Z : Int\n  | S : (x : Int) --> Int\n  | P : (x : Int) --> Int\n  rule : S (P x) <=> x;\n  rule : P (S x) <=> x;\n}\n\n/// Three colours: an enumeration, a data type whose constructors take no\n/// field.\npub data Color : Type where\n  | Red : Color\n  | Green : Color\n  | Blue : Color\n\n/// The two branches of a binary node.\npub data Two : Type where\n  | L : Two\n  | R : Two\n\n/// Lists of elements of a type `a`. The element stands only where a list\n/// holds it, strictly positive, so a family nests through it.\npub data List(a : Type) : Type where\n  | Nil : List(a)\n  | Cons : (head : a, tail : List(a)) --> List(a)\n\n//! The folds over a polymorphic list — its length, appending and mapping — in\n//! the spellings an arm admits, each as the sugared form writes it: a `run`\n//! that only sequences a call, its result returned or the `return` elided, is\n//! written with the call nested in the constructor it returns. A structural\n//! recursive call stands one stage below the list it was read from\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]; the list is the polymorphic one\n//! of Martin-Löf's type theory. A fold may take the element type implicitly,\n//! `[a : Type]`: its recursive call omits `a` and passes the function's own.\n//! They run at the naturals in `recursive-nat-lists.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals and the lists.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n/// The length of a list, returned, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length_elided(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_elided[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the recursive call nested in the successor.\ndef rec length_nested(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_nested[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, its element type implicit: the recursive call omits\n/// `a`, so it passes the function's own, and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec length_implicit[a : Type](xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_implicit[<] rest)\n\n/// Appending two lists, returned, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append_elided(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_elided[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the recursive call nested in the constructor.\ndef rec append_nested(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_nested[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, their element type implicit: the recursive call\n/// passes `a` and `ys` on and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec append_implicit[a : Type](ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_implicit[<] ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, returned, the runs written at their reads.\npub def rec map(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, the `return`s elided, the runs written at\n/// their reads.\ndef rec map_elided(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_elided[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping over a list, both calls nested in the constructor.\ndef rec map_nested(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_nested[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n//! Rose trees: a node holds its children, in two forms. Written directly, a\n//! node holds a `List` of nodes: the list's parameter is strictly positive, so\n//! each child stands below the node and the family declares; a measure recurses\n//! through the list's `map`, which a call instantiates at the children's stage,\n//! and a run passes `map` the children's type as a code. Written as a block, a\n//! node holds a forest and a forest is empty or a tree before a forest: the two\n//! families name each other, so they are one block, admitted as one and sharing\n//! one stage; and the two measures name each other, so they are one fixpoint\n//! over the sum of their domains, each measure that fixpoint at its own\n//! summand. A measure may take a parameter after the tree, and the fixpoint\n//! passes it on. A call through either stands one stage below the node or\n//! forest it was read from [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]. What\n//! the direct form refuses is in `../../pathological/data/rose.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - see: #3722 — a measure passes a parameter after the tree to its sibling,\n//!   by which `treeWeight` and `forestWeight` check\n//! - see: #517 — `Bush`\n//! - see: #393 — `nodes`\n//! - see: #452 — `shrub_nodes`, `grown_nodes`\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals the measures count in, their addition, and the lists the\n// direct form nests through.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n// The lists' `map`, which the direct form's measures recurse through.\nimport \"./recursive-lists.gandr\";\n\n/// The direct form: a node over a list of nodes, nested through `List`'s\n/// strictly positive parameter.\ndata Bush : Type where\n  | Sprout : (kids : List(Bush)) --> Bush\n\n/// A node without children, written directly.\ndef sprig : Bush = Sprout (Nil : List(Bush));\n\n/// A node over two childless nodes, written directly.\ndef shrub : Bush = Sprout (Cons sprig (Cons sprig (Nil : List(Bush)) : List(Bush)) : List(Bush));\n\n/// A list of numbers, summed.\ndef rec sum(xs : List(Nat)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => plus x (sum[<] rest)\n\n/// The nodes of a bush, its children's measured through `map`: the call\n/// instantiates `map`'s element at the children's stage, below the node's,\n/// so `nodes[<]` takes each child.\ndef rec nodes(t : Bush) -> Nat =\n  match t\n    | Sprout(kids) => Succ (sum (map Bush Nat nodes[<] kids))\n\n/// A map over the tree: every node gains a childless node beside its\n/// children, each child grown through `map` at its stage.\ndef rec grow(t : Bush) -> Bush =\n  match t\n    | Sprout(kids) => Sprout (Cons sprig (map Bush Bush grow[<] kids) : List(Bush))\n\n/// The shrub's three nodes, its children counted through `map` at the\n/// children's type.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\ndef shrub_nodes : () -> Nat = nodes shrub;\n\n/// The shrub grown, then counted: the root gains a childless node and each\n/// of its two children gains one, six nodes in all.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))))\") ]\ndef grown_nodes : () -> Nat = nodes (grow shrub);\n\n/// The tree and its forest, one block: each family names the other, and\n/// the two measures recurse through each other.\nrec {\n  /// A node over its forest of children.\n  @[ checks ]\n  data Rose : Type where {\n    | Node : (kids : Forest) --> Rose\n  }\n\n  /// The children of a node, in order.\n  @[ checks ]\n  data Forest : Type where {\n    | Empty : Forest\n    | More : (head : Rose, tail : Forest) --> Forest\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a tree: its own and its children's.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec treeSize(t : Rose) -> Nat {\n    match t\n      | Node(kids) => { let s = forestSize[<] kids; Succ s }\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a forest: each tree's, summed.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec forestSize(ts : Forest) -> Nat =\n    match ts\n      | Empty => Zero\n      | More(t, rest) => { let a = treeSize[<] t; let b = forestSize[<] rest; plus a b }\n}\n\n/// A node without children.\ndef leaf : Rose = Node Empty;\n\n/// A node over two leaves.\ndef sapling : Rose = Node (More leaf (More leaf Empty));\n\n/// The sapling's three nodes.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\ndef sapling_nodes : () -> Nat = treeSize sapling;\n\n/// A forest of a sapling and a leaf: four nodes.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))\") ]\ndef forested : () -> Nat = forestSize (More sapling (More leaf Empty));\n\n/// Two measures that pass a weight to each other unchanged, one block. Each\n/// descends on its first parameter, and the joint passes `k` on.\nrec {\n  /// The nodes of a tree, each weighing `k`.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec treeWeight(t : Rose, k : Nat) -> Nat {\n    match t\n      | Node(kids) => plus k (forestWeight[<] kids k)\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a forest, each weighing `k`.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec forestWeight(ts : Forest, k : Nat) -> Nat =\n    match ts\n      | Empty => Zero\n      | More(t, rest) => plus (treeWeight[<] t k) (forestWeight[<] rest k)\n}\n\n/// The sapling's three nodes, each weighing two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))))\") ]\ndef sapling_weight : () -> Nat = treeWeight sapling (Succ (Succ Zero));\n\n/// Two members that rebuild a tree and its forest, one block. Each member\n/// returns its own family, so the joint's goal differs from member to\n/// member, and a case over the sum computes it.\n///\n/// - see: #3880\nrec {\n  /// A copy of the tree, one node at a time.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec bumpTree(t : Rose) -> Rose {\n    match t\n      | Node(kids) => Node (bumpForest[<] kids)\n  }\n\n  /// A copy of the forest, one tree at a time.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec bumpForest(ts : Forest) -> Forest =\n    match ts\n      | Empty => Empty\n      | More(t, rest) => More (bumpTree[<] t) (bumpForest[<] rest)\n}\n\n/// The sapling's copy keeps its three nodes.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\ndef rebuilt_nodes : () -> Nat = treeSize (bumpTree sapling);\n\n/// The copy of a forest of a sapling and a leaf keeps its four nodes.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))\") ]\ndef rebuilt_forest : () -> Nat = forestSize (bumpForest (More sapling (More leaf Empty)));\n"}, unit={sources:size=4, prelude:Some({0:0})}, base={0:11463}) at typed.rs:1414:5

A numeral annotated\n/// `(n : Nat)` is its constructor term.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  /// Zero.\n  | Zero : Nat\n  /// A positive number.\n  | Positive : (p : Pos) --> Nat\n\n/// A carrier a natural literal reads into: a literal checked where `Carrier`\n/// is expected is `fromNat` of its primitive integer.\npub theory FromNat where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromNat : (n : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n/// A carrier a negative literal reads into: `-n` checked where `Carrier` is\n/// expected is `fromNeg` of the magnitude `n`, at the operand type `fromNat`\n/// takes.\npub theory FromNeg where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromNeg : (n : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n/// A carrier a decimal literal reads into: a decimal checked where `Carrier`\n/// is expected is `fromDecimal` of its exact digits `mantissa` and its\n/// `exponent`, the value `mantissa × 10^exponent`, as Lean's `OfScientific`.\n/// A decimal at `f64` reads as the float it rounds to, through no model.\npub theory FromDecimal where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromDecimal : (mantissa : Integer, exponent : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n//! The data subject's shared declarations, written once and imported by every\n//! source that reads them (`import \"./data-base.gandr\";`): the free naturals,\n//! their addition and the numerals two and three, the integers presented by two\n//! cancelling constructors, a three-colour enumeration, the two branches of a\n//! binary node and the polymorphic lists. A data block is an inductive type\n//! given by its constructors [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory], and a\n//! rule between constructor terms quotients it\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]. The universe subject\n//! reads the naturals and the branches from here too.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]\n//! - cites: [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]\n//!\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-89366-2_16 \"Altenkirch et al., Quotient inductive-inductive types\"\n//! [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]: https://archive-pml.github.io/martin-lof/pdfs/Bibliopolis-Book-retypeset-1984.pdf \"Martin-Löf, Intuitionistic Type Theory\"\n\n/// Natural numbers, free: every constructor term is its own value.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  | Zero : Nat\n  | Succ : (n : Nat) --> Nat\n\n/// Addition by recursion on the second argument, the recursive call nested\n/// in the successor it returns.\npub def rec plus(n : Nat, m : Nat) -> Nat =\n  match m\n    | Zero => n\n    | Succ(p) => Succ (plus[<] n p)\n\n/// Two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ Zero)\") ]\npub def two : Nat = Succ (Succ Zero);\n\n/// Three.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\npub def three : Nat = Succ two;\n\n/// The integers as a presented type: a successor and a predecessor that\n/// cancel, each rule invertible.\n@[ normalizes(\"S(P(S(Z))) ~> S(Z)\") ]\npub data Int : Type where {\n  | Z : Int\n  | S : (x : Int) --> Int\n  | P : (x : Int) --> Int\n  rule : S (P x) <=> x;\n  rule : P (S x) <=> x;\n}\n\n/// Three colours: an enumeration, a data type whose constructors take no\n/// field.\npub data Color : Type where\n  | Red : Color\n  | Green : Color\n  | Blue : Color\n\n/// The two branches of a binary node.\npub data Two : Type where\n  | L : Two\n  | R : Two\n\n/// Lists of elements of a type `a`. The element stands only where a list\n/// holds it, strictly positive, so a family nests through it.\npub data List(a : Type) : Type where\n  | Nil : List(a)\n  | Cons : (head : a, tail : List(a)) --> List(a)\n\n//! The folds over a polymorphic list — its length, appending and mapping — in\n//! the spellings an arm admits, each as the sugared form writes it: a `run`\n//! that only sequences a call, its result returned or the `return` elided, is\n//! written with the call nested in the constructor it returns. A structural\n//! recursive call stands one stage below the list it was read from\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]; the list is the polymorphic one\n//! of Martin-Löf's type theory. A fold may take the element type implicitly,\n//! `[a : Type]`: its recursive call omits `a` and passes the function's own.\n//! They run at the naturals in `recursive-nat-lists.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals and the lists.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n/// The length of a list, returned, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length_elided(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_elided[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the recursive call nested in the successor.\ndef rec length_nested(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_nested[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, its element type implicit: the recursive call omits\n/// `a`, so it passes the function's own, and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec length_implicit[a : Type](xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_implicit[<] rest)\n\n/// Appending two lists, returned, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append_elided(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_elided[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the recursive call nested in the constructor.\ndef rec append_nested(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_nested[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, their element type implicit: the recursive call\n/// passes `a` and `ys` on and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec append_implicit[a : Type](ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_implicit[<] ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, returned, the runs written at their reads.\npub def rec map(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, the `return`s elided, the runs written at\n/// their reads.\ndef rec map_elided(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_elided[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping over a list, both calls nested in the constructor.\ndef rec map_nested(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_nested[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n//! Rose trees: a node holds its children, in two forms. Written directly, a\n//! node holds a `List` of nodes: the list's parameter is strictly positive, so\n//! each child stands below the node and the family declares; a measure recurses\n//! through the list's `map`, which a call instantiates at the children's stage,\n//! and a run passes `map` the children's type as a code. Written as a block, a\n//! node holds a forest and a forest is empty or a tree before a forest: the two\n//! families name each other, so they are one block, admitted as one and sharing\n//! one stage; and the two measures name each other, so they are one fixpoint\n//! over the sum of their domains, each measure that fixpoint at its own\n//! summand. A measure may take a parameter after the tree, and the fixpoint\n//! passes it on. A call through either stands one stage below the node or\n//! forest it was read from [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]. What\n//! the direct form refuses is in `../../pathological/data/rose.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - see: #3722 — a measure passes a parameter after the tree to its sibling,\n//!   by which `treeWeight` and `forestWeight` check\n//! - see: #517 — `Bush`\n//! - see: #393 — `nodes`\n//! - see: #452 — `shrub_nodes`, `grown_nodes`\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals the measures count in, their addition, and the lists the\n// direct form nests through.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n// The lists' `map`, which the direct form's measures recurse through.\nimport \"./recursive-lists.gandr\";\n\n/// The direct form: a node over a list of nodes, nested through `List`'s\n/// strictly positive parameter.\ndata Bush : Type where\n  | Sprout : (kids : List(Bush)) --> Bush\n\n/// A node without children, written directly.\ndef sprig : Bush = Sprout (Nil : List(Bush));\n\n/// A node over two childless nodes, written directly.\ndef shrub : Bush = Sprout (Cons sprig (Cons sprig (Nil : List(Bush)) : List(Bush)) : List(Bush));\n\n/// A list of numbers, summed.\ndef rec sum(xs : List(Nat)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => plus x (sum[<] rest)\n\n/// The nodes of a bush, its children's measured through `map`: the call\n/// instantiates `map`'s element at the children's stage, below the node's,\n/// so `nodes[<]` takes each child.\ndef rec nodes(t : Bush) -> Nat =\n  match t\n    | Sprout(kids) => Succ (sum (map Bush Nat nodes[<] kids))\n\n/// A map over the tree: every node gains a childless node beside its\n/// children, each child grown through `map` at its stage.\ndef rec grow(t : Bush) -> Bush =\n  match t\n    | Sprout(kids) => Sprout (Cons sprig (map Bush Bush grow[<] kids) : List(Bush))\n\n/// The shrub's three nodes, its children counted through each\n/// of its two children gains one, six nodes in all.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))))\") ]\ndef grown_nodes : () -> Nat = nodes (grow shrub);\n\n/// The tree and its forest, one block: each family names the other, and\n/// the two measures recurse through each other.\nrec {\n  /// A node over its forest of children.\n  @[ checks ]\n  data Rose : Type where {\n    | Node : (kids : Forest) --> Rose\n  }\n\n  /// The children of a node, in order.\n  @[ checks ]\n  data Forest : Type where {\n    | Empty : Forest\n    | More : (head : Rose, tail : Forest) --> Forest\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a tree: its own and its children's.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec treeSize(t : Rose) -> Nat {\n    match t\n      | Node(kids) => { let s = forestSize[<] kids; Succ s }\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a forest: each tree's, summed.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec forestSize(ts : Forest) -> Nat =\n    match ts\n      | Empty => Zero\n      | More(t, rest) => { let a = treeSize[<] Empty));\n\n/// The sapling's three nodes.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\ndef sapling_nodes : () -> Nat = treeSize sapling;\n\n/// A forest of a sapling and a leaf: four nodes.\n@[ Each\n/// descends on its first parameter, and the joint passes `k` on.\nrec {\n  /// The nodes of a tree, each weighing `k`.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec treeWeight(t : Rose, k : Nat) -> Nat {\n    match t\n      | Node(kids) => plus k (forestWeight[<] kids k)\n  }\n\n  /// The nodes of a forest, each weighing `k`.\n  @[ checks ]\n  def rec forestWeight(ts : Forest, k : Nat) -> Nat =\n    match ts\n      | Empty => Zero\n      | More(t, rest) => plus (treeWeight[<] t k) (forestWeight[<] rest k)\n}\n\n/// The sapling's three nodes, each weighing two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ (Succ Zero)))))\") ]\ndef sapling_weight : () -> Nat = treeWeight sapling (Succ (Succ Zero));\n\n/// Two members that rebuild a tree and its forest, one block. The data subject's shared declarations, written once and imported by every\n//! source that reads them (`import \"./data-base.gandr\";`): the free naturals,\n//! their addition and the numerals two and three, the integers presented by two\n//! cancelling constructors, a three-colour enumeration, the two branches of a\n//! binary node and the polymorphic lists. A data block is an inductive type\n//! given by its constructors [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory], and a\n//! rule between constructor terms quotients it\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]. The universe subject\n//! reads the naturals and the branches from here too.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]\n//! - cites: [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]\n//!\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-89366-2_16 \"Altenkirch et al., Quotient inductive-inductive types\"\n//! [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]: https://archive-pml.github.io/martin-lof/pdfs/Bibliopolis-Book-retypeset-1984.pdf \"Martin-Löf, Intuitionistic Type Theory\"\n\n/// Natural numbers, free: every constructor term is its own value.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  | Zero : Nat\n  | Succ : (n : Nat) --> Nat\n\n/// Addition by recursion on the second argument, the recursive call nested\n/// in the successor it returns.\npub def rec plus(n : Nat, m : Nat) -> Nat =\n  match m\n    | Zero => n\n    | Succ(p) => Succ (plus[<] n p)\n\n/// Two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ Zero)\") ]\npub def two : Nat = Succ (Succ Zero);\n\n/// Three.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\npub def three : Nat = Succ two;\n\n/// The integers as a presented type: a successor and a predecessor that\n/// cancel, each rule invertible.\n@[ normalizes(\"S(P(S(Z))) ~> S(Z)\") ]\npub data Int : Type where {\n  | Z : Int\n  | S : (x : Int) --> Int\n  | P : (x : Int) --> Int\n  rule : S (P x) <=> x;\n  rule : P (S x) <=> x;\n}\n\n/// Three colours: an enumeration, a data type whose constructors take no\n/// field.\npub data Color : Type where\n  | Red : Color\n  | Green : Color\n  | Blue : Color\n\n/// The two branches of a binary node.\npub data Two : Type where\n  | L : Two\n  | R : Two\n\n/// Lists of elements of a type `a`. The folds over a polymorphic list — its length, appending and mapping — in\n//! the spellings an arm admits, each as the sugared form writes it: a `run`\n//! that only sequences a call, its result returned or the `return` elided, is\n//! written with the call nested in the constructor it returns. A structural\n//! recursive call stands one stage below the list it was read from\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]; the list is the polymorphic one\n//! of Martin-Löf's type theory. A fold may take the element type implicitly,\n//! `[a : Type]`: its recursive call omits `a` and passes the function's own.\n//! They run at the naturals in `recursive-nat-lists.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals and the lists.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n/// The length of a list, returned, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length_elided(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ rest) => Succ (length_nested[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, its element type implicit: the recursive call omits\n/// `a`, so it passes the function's own, and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec length_implicit[a : Type](xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_implicit[<] rest)\n\n/// Appending two lists, returned, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append_elided(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_elided[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the recursive call nested in the constructor.\ndef rec append_nested(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n def rec append_implicit[a : Type](ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_implicit[<] ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, returned, the runs written at their reads.\npub def rec map(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, the `return`s elided, the runs written at\n/// their reads.\ndef rec map_elided(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_elided[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping over a list, both calls nested in the constructor.\ndef rec map_nested(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_nested[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n//! Rose trees: a node holds its children, in two forms. Written directly, a\n//! node holds a `List` of nodes: the list's parameter is strictly positive, so\n//! each child stands below the node and the family declares; a measure recurses\n//! through the list's `map`, which a call instantiates at the children's stage,\n//! and a run passes `map` the children's type as a code. Written as a block, a\n//! node holds a forest and a forest is empty or a tree before a forest: the two\n//! families name each other, so they are one block, admitted as one and sharing\n//! one stage; and the two measures name each other, so they are one fixpoint\n//! over the sum of their domains, each measure that fixpoint at its own\n//! summand. A measure may take a parameter after the tree, and the fixpoint\n//! passes it on. A call through either stands one stage below the node or\n//! forest it was read from [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]. What\n//! the direct form refuses is in `../../pathological/data/rose.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - see: #3722 — a measure passes a parameter after the tree to its sibling,\n//!   by which `treeWeight` and `forestWeight` check\n//! - see: #517 — `Bush`\n//! - see: #393 — `nodes`\n//! - see: #452 — `shrub_nodes`, `grown_nodes`\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals the measures count in, their addition, and the lists the\n// direct form nests through.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n// The lists' `map`, which the direct form's measures recurse through.\nimport \"./recursive-lists.gandr\";\n\n/// The direct form: a node over a list of nodes, nested through `List`'s\n/// strictly positive parameter.\ndata Bush : Type where\n  | Sprout : (kids : List(Bush)) --> Bush\n\n/// A node without children, written directly.\ndef sprig : Bush = Sprout (Nil : List(Bush));\n\n/// A node over two childless nodes, written directly.\ndef shrub : Bush = Sprout (Cons sprig (Cons sprig (Nil : List(Bush)) : List(Bush)) : List(Bush));\n\n/// A list of numbers, summed.\ndef rec sum(xs : List(Nat)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => plus x (sum[<] rest)\n\n/// The nodes of a bush, its children's measured through `map`: the call\n/// instantiates `map`'s element at the children's stage, below the node's,\n/// so `nodes[<]` takes each child.\ndef rec nodes(t : Bush) -> Nat =\n  match module EqInteger : Model(Eq) with sort Carrier = Integer where\n  /// Whether two integers are equal.\n  def eq(a : Integer, b : Integer) -> Bool { int.eq a b }\n  law refl = ~admitted;\n  law sym = ~admitted;\n\n/// String equality, the string primitive, its laws the host's.\npub module EqString : Model(Eq) with sort Carrier = String where\n  /// Whether two strings are equal, byte for byte.\n  def eq(a : String, b : String) -> Bool { string.eq a b }\n  law refl = ~admitted;\n  law sym = ~admitted;\n\n/// Float equality, the float primitive: symmetric, and no equality, since\n/// NaN is equal to nothing, itself included.\npub module PartialEqFloat : Model(PartialEq) with sort Carrier = f64 where\n  /// Whether two floats are equal, NaN equal to none.\n  def eq(a : f64, b : f64) -> Bool { float64.eq a b }\n  law sym = ~admitted;\n\n/// Equality through the model of `PartialEq` the operands' type resolves:\n/// what `a == b` calls.\npub def eq[e : Model(PartialEq)](a : e.Carrier, b : e.Carrier) -> Bool { e.eq a b }\n\n/// A decidable order over a carrier with its equality: below, at most, above\n/// and at least.\npub theory Ord where\n  include Eq;\n  oper lt : (a : Carrier, b : Carrier) --> Bool;\n  oper le : (a : Carrier, b : Carrier) --> Bool;\n  oper gt : (a : Carrier, b : Carrier) --> Bool;\n  oper ge : (a : Carrier, b : Carrier) --> Bool;\n\n/// The integers' order, the integer primitives, over `EqInteger`.\npub module OrdInteger : Model(Ord) with sort Carrier = Integer where\n  include EqInteger;\n  /// Whether one integer is below another.\n  def lt(a : Integer, b : Integer) -> Bool { int.lt a b }\n  /// Whether one integer is at most another.\n  def le(a : Integer, b : Integer) -> Bool { int.le a b }\n  /// Whether one integer is above another.\n  def gt(a : Integer, b : Integer) -> Bool { int.gt a b }\n  /// Whether one integer is at least another.\n  def ge(a : Integer, b : Integer) -> Bool { int.ge a b }\n\n/// The strings' byte-lexicographic order, the string primitive, over\n/// `EqString`.\npub module OrdString : Model(Ord) with sort Carrier = String where\n  include EqString;\n  /// Whether one string is below another, byte by byte.\n  def lt(a : String, b : String) -> Bool { string.lt a b }\n  /// Whether one string is at most another.\n  def le(a : String, b : String) -> Bool { bool.not (string.lt b a) }\n  /// Whether one string is above another.\n  def gt(a : String, b : String) -> Bool { string.lt b a }\n  /// Whether one string is at least another.\n  def ge(a : String, b : String) -> Bool { bool.not (string.lt a b) }\n\n/// Below, through the model of `Ord` the operands' type resolves: what `a < b`\n/// calls.\npub def lt[o : Model(Ord)](a : o.Carrier, b : o.Carrier) -> Bool { o.lt a b }\n\n/// At most, through the operands' model of `Ord`: what `a <= b` calls.\npub def le[o : Model(Ord)](a : o.Carrier, b : o.Carrier) -> Bool { o.le a b }\n\n/// Above, through the operands' model of `Ord`: what `a > b` calls.\npub def gt[o : Model(Ord)](a : o.Carrier, b : o.Carrier) -> Bool { o.gt a b }\n\n/// At least, through the operands' model of `Ord`: what `a >= b` calls.\npub def ge[o : Model(Ord)](a : o.Carrier, b : o.Carrier) -> Bool { o.ge a b }\n\n/// A positive number in binary, its least digit outermost, as Isabelle/HOL's\n/// `num`: one, or twice a positive, or twice a positive and one.\npub data Pos : Type where\n  /// One.\n  | One : Pos\n  /// Twice `p`.\n  | Bit0 : (p : Pos) --> Pos\n  /// Twice `p`, and one.\n  | Bit1 : (p : Pos) --> Pos\n\n/// A natural number in binary: zero, or a positive. A numeral annotated\n/// `(n : Nat)` is its constructor term.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  /// Zero.\n  | Zero : Nat\n  /// A positive number.\n  | Positive : (p : Pos) --> Nat\n\n/// A carrier a natural literal reads into: a literal checked where `Carrier`\n/// is expected is `fromNat` of its primitive integer.\npub theory FromNat where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromNat : (n : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n/// A carrier a negative literal reads into: `-n` checked where `Carrier` is\n/// expected is `fromNeg` of the magnitude `n`, at the operand type `fromNat`\n/// takes.\npub theory FromNeg where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromNeg : (n : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n/// A carrier a decimal literal reads into: a decimal checked where `Carrier`\n/// is expected is `fromDecimal` of its exact digits `mantissa` and its\n/// `exponent`, the value `mantissa × 10^exponent`, as Lean's `OfScientific`.\n/// A decimal at `f64` reads as the float it rounds to, through no model.\npub theory FromDecimal where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromDecimal : (mantissa : Integer, exponent : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n//! The data subject's shared declarations, written once and imported by every\n//! source that reads them (`import \"./data-base.gandr\";`): the free naturals,\n//! their addition and the numerals two and three, the integers presented by two\n//! cancelling constructors, a three-colour enumeration, the two branches of a\n//! binary node and the polymorphic lists. A data block is an inductive type\n//! given by its constructors [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory], and a\n//! rule between constructor terms quotients it\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]. The universe subject\n//! reads the naturals and the branches from here too.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]\n//! - cites: [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]\n//!\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-89366-2_16 \"Altenkirch et al., Quotient inductive-inductive types\"\n//! [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]: https://archive-pml.github.io/martin-lof/pdfs/Bibliopolis-Book-retypeset-1984.pdf \"Martin-Löf, Intuitionistic Type Theory\"\n\n/// Natural numbers, free: every constructor term is its own value.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  | Zero : Nat\n  | Succ : (n : Nat) --> Nat\n\n/// Addition by recursion on the second argument, the recursive call nested\n/// in the successor it returns.\npub def rec plus(n : Nat, m : Nat) -> Nat =\n  match m\n    | Zero => n\n    | Succ(p) => Succ (plus[<] n p)\n\n/// Two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ Zero)\") ]\npub def two : Nat = Succ (Succ Zero);\n\n/// Three.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\npub def three : Nat = Succ two;\n\n/// The integers as a presented type: a successor and a predecessor that\n/// cancel, each rule invertible.\n@[ normalizes(\"S(P(S(Z))) ~> S(Z)\") ]\npub data Int : Type where {\n  | Z : Int\n  | S : (x : Int) --> Int\n  | P : (x : Int) --> Int\n  rule : S (P x) <=> x;\n  rule : P (S x) <=> x;\n}\n\n/// Three colours: an enumeration, a data type whose constructors take no\n/// field.\npub data Color : Type where\n  | Red : Color\n  | Green : Color\n  | Blue : Color\n\n/// The two branches of a binary node.\npub data Two : Type where\n  | L : Two\n  | R : Two\n\n/// Lists of elements of a type `a`. The element stands only where a list\n/// holds it, strictly positive, so a family nests through it.\npub data List(a : Type) : Type where\n  | Nil : List(a)\n  | Cons : (head : a, tail : List(a)) --> List(a)\n\n//! The folds over a polymorphic list — its length, appending and mapping — in\n//! the spellings an arm admits, each as the sugared form writes it: a `run`\n//! that only sequences a call, its result returned or the `return` elided, is\n//! written with the call nested in the constructor it returns. A structural\n//! recursive call stands one stage below the list it was read from\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]; the list is the polymorphic one\n//! of Martin-Löf's type theory. A fold may take the element type implicitly,\n//! `[a : Type]`: its recursive call omits `a` and passes the function's own.\n//! They run at the naturals in `recursive-nat-lists.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals and the lists.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n/// The length of a list, returned, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\npub def rec length_elided(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_elided[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, the recursive call nested in the successor.\ndef rec length_nested(a : Type, xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_nested[<] a rest)\n\n/// The length of a list, its element type implicit: the recursive call omits\n/// `a`, so it passes the function's own, and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec length_implicit[a : Type](xs : List(a)) -> Nat =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => Zero\n    | Cons(x, rest) => Succ (length_implicit[<] rest)\n\n/// Appending two lists, returned, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the `return`s elided, the run written at its read.\ndef rec append_elided(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_elided[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, the recursive call nested in the constructor.\ndef rec append_nested(a : Type, ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_nested[<] a ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Appending two lists, their element type implicit: the recursive call\n/// passes `a` and `ys` on and descends on `xs`.\npub def rec append_implicit[a : Type](ys : List(a), xs : List(a)) -> List(a) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => ys\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons x (append_implicit[<] ys rest) : List(a))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, returned, the runs written at their reads.\npub def rec map(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping a function over a list, the `return`s elided, the runs written at\n/// their reads.\ndef rec map_elided(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_elided[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n/// Mapping over a list, both calls nested in the constructor.\ndef rec map_nested(a : Type, b : Type, f : a -> b, xs : List(a)) -> List(b) =\n  match xs\n    | Nil => (Nil : List(b))\n    | Cons(x, rest) => (Cons (f x) (map_nested[<] a b f rest) : List(b))\n\n//! Rose trees: a node holds its children, in two forms. Written directly, a\n//! node holds a `List` of nodes: the list's parameter is strictly positive, so\n//! each child stands below the node and the family declares; a measure recurses\n//! through the list's `map`, which a call instantiates at the children's stage,\n//! and a run passes `map` the children's type as a code. Written as a block, a\n//! node holds a forest and a forest is empty or a tree before a forest: the two\n//! families name each other, so they are one block, admitted as one and sharing\n//! one stage; and the two measures name each other, so they are one fixpoint\n//! over the sum of their domains, each measure that fixpoint at its own\n//! summand. A measure may take a parameter after the tree, and the fixpoint\n//! passes it on. A call through either stands one stage below the node or\n//! forest it was read from [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]. What\n//! the direct form refuses is in `../../pathological/data/rose.gandr`.\n//!\n//! - see: #3722 — a measure passes a parameter after the tree to its sibling,\n//!   by which `treeWeight` and `forestWeight` check\n//! - see: #517 — `Bush`\n//! - see: #393 — `nodes`\n//! - see: #452 — `shrub_nodes`, `grown_nodes`\n//! - cites: [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]\n//!\n//! [Abel-2004-termination_checking_with_types]: https://doi.org/10.1051/ita:2004015 \"Abel, Termination checking with types\"\n\n// The naturals the measures count in, their addition, and the lists the\n// direct form nests through.\nimport \"./data-base.gandr\";\n\n// The lists' `map`, which the direct form's measures recurse through.\nimport \"./recursive-lists.gandr\";\n\n/// The direct form:
